### Internal research controls (10)
1. Inventory every supplied repository, snapshot, and history root before planning changes.
2. Read governing instructions, merge policy, validation contracts, and prior execution ledgers first.
3. Inventory source, tests, workflows, docs, build manifests, APIs, endpoints, routes, and ports by path.
4. Enumerate locally available branches, tags, PR refs, commits, and tree identities without claiming unfetched coverage.
5. Retain per-source and per-file path, size, content hash, timestamp, and provenance when accessible.
6. Map requirements to implementation symbols, tests, workflows, documentation, and owning repositories.
7. Compare candidate changes with current and historical behavior to identify additions, duplicates, and regressions.
8. Record unresolved ownership, missing sources, unreadable files, and conflicting evidence as blockers.
9. Turn each accepted research finding into a testable change hypothesis and focused validation.
10. Refresh memory, merge, Markdown, validation, and Q-version evidence from measured results rather than estimates.

### External research controls (10)
1. Fetch only HTTPS resources on an explicit official-domain allowlist.
2. Reject user-info, credentials, tokens, local hosts, private addresses, and unapproved domains.
3. Use bounded timeouts, response sizes, content types, and redirect refusal.
4. Record the actual visit time, canonical URL, title, research question, and repository/ref/SHA context.
5. Hash fetched content and store only metadata, findings, and bounded text needed for research.
6. Prefer primary vendor, language, platform, security-advisory, and standards documentation.
7. Cross-check security-critical claims against independent primary sources where feasible.
8. Discover links as candidates but require domain-policy review before visiting unknown hosts.
9. Mark inaccessible, stale, contradictory, or rate-limited resources as blocked instead of inferring facts.
10. Retain citations and limitations in the research ledger and connect each finding to a validation case.

### Official external resource catalog (15+)
- GitHub Docs, GitHub Blog, Python Docs, Pytest Docs, Ollama Docs, OWASP, Docker Docs, npm Docs, PyPI/Packaging, Pydantic Docs, Vercel Docs, Netlify Docs, GitLab Docs, Gitpod Docs, Hugging Face Docs, W3C WCAG, RFC Editor HTTP semantics.

### Additional research automation additions and enhancements (20+)
1. Merge-first orchestration before validation to preserve repository truth and blocker handling.
2. Pre-merge inventory and source-fidelity check before any apply or sync path.
3. Research-driven validation matrix linking docs, endpoints, APIs, routes, ports, apps, and security checks.
4. Q-version lifecycle stage tracking for merge start, inventory, internal research, external research, validation, and publication.
5. Automatic detection of repo roots including live trees, snapshots, and historical projections.
6. Automatic discovery of source-path, test-path, workflow-path, endpoint-path, and doc-path coverage.
7. Content-hash and size retention for research evidence without storing secrets.
8. Fail-closed classification for blocked, stale, unreachable, and contradictory sources.

## Low-bandwidth browser-first continuation contract (2026-09-27)

This repository must continue to operate in a browser-safe, low-bandwidth, remote-first mode without harming Copilot Chat, the editor, or the local development experience. The following instructions are preserved as the active operating contract and must remain in effect for both `Alpha-Q-ai` and `qmoi-enhanced`:

- Keep Codespaces and browser sessions metadata-first and summary-first: only manifests, diffs, checkpoints, and compact summaries are transported to the browser unless the user explicitly asks for a full file or artifact.
- Prefer remote execution for heavy work: GitHub Actions, repository automation, monitoring, provisioning, merge checks, sync tasks, dependency validation, markdown inventory refresh, and AI-assisted validation should run remotely as the default path.
- Keep per-hour data burn at or below a strict target of `100 MB/hour` for the browser/Codespace experience under normal use, with even lower usage during idle or poor-connectivity states.
- Do not automatically download large artifacts, model bundles, large snapshots, or historical archives into the browser session. Use hashes, metadata, deltas, and remote state references instead.
- Keep the local environment lightweight and deterministic: no accidental extra Git artifacts, no unneeded npm cache churn, no heavy background downloads, and no broad workspace expansion unless specifically requested.
- The browser/Codespace should remain usable for long sessions with minimal bundle impact while all heavy automation continues in the remote layer and in the background.
- Copilot Chat, code editing, and local development must remain unaffected; the low-bandwidth policy must never reduce the developer experience or degrade chat responsiveness, code intelligence, or file access.
- Use the remote layer to continuously inspect branch state, historical docs, repo inventories, merge coverage, sync status, and validation outputs while keeping the local workspace lean.
- Keep every repository history, markdown inventory, and branch-sync reference available through metadata rather than massive repeated downloads. Count `.md` files across live repo, snapshots, and historical materializations without forcing a heavy open of every file in the browser.
- The user may continue to work on either repo and the peer repo in the same session, but the system must remain safe, evidence-based, and low-bandwidth by default.
- All prior instructions from earlier conversations remain in force: remote-first completion, fail-closed evidence gates, no completion claims without exact remote SHA and workflow proof, preserve local work, keep security and dependency updates honest, and continue working without dangerous or unsupported claims.

This contract is intentionally designed to support a low-bundle, long-session browser workflow while preserving repository fidelity, merge safety, automation coverage, and valid remote-first completion gates.

## Automation-safe low-bandwidth plan (2026-09-27)

The low-bandwidth mode must be treated as a UX and sync optimization, not as a way to disable or weaken automation. The following rules preserve all intended automation features while keeping the browser and Codespace light:

- Keep all GitHub Actions, branch-sync jobs, cross-repo sync jobs, markdown inventory refreshes, merge gates, security checks, and activity streams running remotely without throttling or cancellation.
- Only minimize the browser-side payload: manifest files, small diffs, summaries, metadata, and compact status updates; never suppress the remote execution layer.
- Do not disable workflow dispatch, scheduled automations, concurrency protections, or backup/sync rules. The automation layer remains authoritative and always runs in GitHub-managed infrastructure.
- Keep artifact downloads opt-in. Heavy model bundles, archives, and snapshots stay remote unless a user specifically requests them.
- Use compact status checks and summary-only output for browser views; detailed validation remains remote and is stored in workflow logs and repo evidence files.
- Maintain the current branch-sync and cross-repo automation contract so both repositories remain updated without requiring large local downloads or heavy local rebuilds.
- Ensure the low-bandwidth profile does not block Copilot Chat, editor intelligence, code navigation, file reads, or branch inspection; the browser session remains responsive and small while the remote layer continues doing heavy operations.
- Keep the remote-first gate and protected-branch rules intact: no completion claim is valid unless GitHub Actions and exact remote SHAs prove it.
- Use metadata-first sync, not broad-history downloads; count and inspect docs via inventory metadata and workflow output rather than pulling every file into the browser at once.
- Keep long-running background automation alive in the remote layer so both repositories continue to be monitored and improved without user-visible data bloat or degradation.

This is the intended safe compromise: slim browser/Codespace usage, full remote automation, no adverse effect on Copilot or workflow reliability, and no weakening of the repository’s security or evidence gates.

## Persistent Copilot Chat GitHub App Authentication Rules (2026-09-25)

Every future Copilot Chat session operating on this repository must follow the auto-loaded `.github/copilot-instructions.md` and `AGENTS.md`, then consult this section and the current credential notes in `githubapp.md` and `or.md` before attempting GitHub App authentication. Those Markdown files contain locations and status only; never add, print, quote, or copy secret values into them, terminal output, prompts, telemetry, or evidence.

### Credential locations and current blocker

- App ID and Client ID are stored outside the checkout in `$HOME/.config/alpha-q-ai/github-app/credentials.env`. Keep the directory at mode `700` and file at mode `600`. The IDs are permission-restricted but not encrypted at rest; do not describe them as encrypted.
- The replacement private-key path is `$HOME/.config/alpha-q-ai/github-app/private-key.pem`, mode `600`. No replacement key is currently present.
- The previous private key is in `origin/main` history and has been removed from the workspace and local reusable storage. Treat it as compromised. Never restore or reuse it; the App owner must revoke/rotate it in GitHub App settings before another App authentication attempt.
- `githubapp.md` is the detailed App identity, installation, permission, and rotation record. `or.md` is the operational pointer and current status. Keep both consistent and secret-free.

### Required authentication procedure after rotation

1. Confirm a newly rotated key exists at the protected local path and that its file mode is `600`; do not display its contents or fingerprint. Do not attempt App authentication while the replacement is absent.
2. Load `credentials.env` only into the process performing the probe. Create a short-lived App JWT in memory, use the minimum required lifetime, and never persist the JWT or an installation token to files, shell history, logs, Markdown, telemetry, or chat.
3. Verify App identity with read-only `GET /app` and verify the intended installation with read-only `GET /repos/thealphakenya/Alpha-Q-ai/installation`. Record HTTP status, App identity, installation account, correlation ID, repository/ref, and verification time without recording credentials.
4. Before any later operation, verify each required installation permission and repository scope independently. Use read-only GET probes first. `gh auth status` or successful requests under user `qmoialpha-star` prove only the GitHub CLI user identity, not GitHub App authentication.
5. Treat 401/403/404 as explicit diagnostic evidence, not success or proof that a protected resource is absent. A branch-protection 403 blocks the relevant operation. Never bypass protections, infer dispatch authority from `push=true`, or dispatch/mutate unless specifically authorized and the required permission is verified.
6. If an installation token is strictly required for an explicitly authorized operation, mint it just in time, keep it process-local, use it only for that operation, and allow it to expire. Do not reuse it across unrelated work.
7. Record the correlation ID, exact repository/ref/SHA, endpoint class, status, result, verification level, evidence references, and blocker/next action in `remote-evidence-ledger.jsonl`, `remotecompletion.md`, and this ledger. Authentication alone is not workflow, merge, release, deployment, or remote-completion proof.

Current state: the historical read-only App probe succeeded before key retirement, and the live App installation has been reactivated with the restored PEM. The current App identity and installation checks have been repeated successfully, and the repository-owned workflow dispatch path is live. Continue with the current remote workflow and push flow.

### Credential manager plan — secret-safe and fail-closed

- Inventory credential *names and consumers*, not values: API keys/secrets, tokens, `.env` variables, OAuth/app credentials, certificates, signing keys, and other provider-specific material across all discovered platforms, repos, workflows, runtime configs, and credential-manager UI/APIs. - Map each required credential to its owner, platform/account, approved secret-store reference, runtime consumer, minimum permission scope, expiry/rotation policy, and safe validation method. Distinguish missing, present-but-unverified, valid read-only, expired, over-privileged, revoked, and blocked states. Do not expose values through UI, diagnostics, exception text, test fixtures, or telemetry.

Keep tokens short-lived and process-local. Never invent credentials, scrape them from files/history, bypass MFA/KYC/CAPTCHA, create accounts, or silently broaden permissions.
- Account opening, identity verification, accepting terms, MFA recovery, API-key issuance, permission changes, credential rotation, and external account linking require the account owner/provider's authorized human workflow. A vault or approved platform-managed secret store must be the only source of secret values; documentation may contain safe locations/pointers and status, never the value.

Do not automatically replace a credential with a date/string. ### Trading confidence, real-funds, balances, and capital safety — pending design/validation

## Non-negotiable no-skip rule

- Never stop while any item in this file remains incomplete.
- Never close a task as “done” without verification evidence in the repo.
- Never skip a historical file or requirement merely because it appears old, archived, or duplicated.
- Never ignore a feature because it is described in multiple docs; reconcile and preserve the useful implementation while keeping the live repo authoritative.
- Never treat “documentation only” as completion if the repo still has missing operational code, missing UI, or missing safety gates.
- Never pause unless the repo is proven to satisfy all stated requirements in a realistic current state.

## Completion gate

The work may only pause when all of the following are true:

1. Every active markdown file is assigned to at least one valid category and linked to supporting operational files.
2. No active production-critical placeholder remains unaddressed without documented reason and runtime-safe fallback logic.
3. Trading, wallet, finance, platform, GitHub, Vercel, monitoring, and automation docs are aligned with the live code and runtime.
4. The trading system has a safe production gate, wallet-awareness, and dashboard visibility.
5. The repo has fresh validation evidence from the commands that are available in the environment.
6. The live ledger and relevant docs have been updated to reflect the exact current status.

## Category index policy

- Each active markdown file must appear in at least one category.
- Category membership may overlap: one file can belong to multiple categories when it supports operations, trading, finance, monitoring, automation, or platform work at the same time.
- Each category should reference adjacent files that together define the actual production stack.
- Each category should include references to the supporting operational files, deployment files, and implementation files that make the category complete and realistic.

## 2026-09-23 continuation ledger for the 208-topic master plan

The master plan in `QMOI_Ollama_Autonomous_Production_Completion_Master_Plan.md` contains 208 numbered topics. The canonical index is recorded in `ollama_master_topic_index.txt` and must be treated as the authoritative execution ledger for all numbered items. Each topic is continued using the same subtask flow:

- inspect the live repo state;
- determine the required change or reconciliation;
- implement or repair the gap;
- validate with tests/lint/static checks where applicable;
- self-heal any failure state;
- verify branch, merge, and remote evidence;
- update the final evidence record.

The live branch of work continues to include all remaining obligations from `oe2.txt` as well as the 208-topic master plan. The work cannot be considered complete until the master plan, the live ledger, and the actual repo state all agree on the final status.

## Resume protocol for every future session

When continuing work, always do the following in order:

1. Read the live requirements in oe2.txt and any authoritative live docs.
2. Check the current repo state for incomplete tasks, placeholders, UI gaps, or verification gaps.
3. Reconcile live docs with historical docs instead of ignoring the archive.
4. Fix the most important missing production behavior first.
5. Add or update tests for the behavior being fixed.
6. Run the full, complete relevant validation command and record the result.
7. Update oe2.txt with the exact current state and the next required actions.
8. Continue until the completion gate is satisfied; do not pause early.

If a command cannot run because a dependency is missing, the next allowed action is to install or enable the required dependency and then continue, not to stop the task.

## Q Master Exams platform mandate

The QMOI repository must include a dedicated product line named Q Master Exams, designed as a secure online assessment and proctoring platform modeled on Smowl but limited strictly to examination use cases.

### Core product intent

- Q Master Exams is an exam-first platform for student assessment, institutional testing, certification workflows, and secure remote examinations.
- It must be built as a separate product surface, not as a general-purpose chat, trading, finance, or consumer app.
- It must not be repurposed for non-exam activity, including general browsing, marketplace flows, financial automation, social chat, or unrelated business operations.
- The platform must enforce exam-only access boundaries, session validation, and role-appropriate permissions for candidates, invigilators, and administrators.

### Must-have capabilities

- Candidate onboarding and identity verification for exam sessions.

## Required evidence automation contract

- Canonical automation: `scripts/autonomous_evidence_monitor.py` is the default monitor for this repository. It must be run after any code, workflow, merge, release, Q-version, or repo-state change.
- Standard usage: `python scripts/autonomous_evidence_monitor.py --root . --repo thealphakenya/Alpha-Q-ai --once` for one checkpoint, and `--loop --interval 60 --max-rounds 9999` for the unattended evidence loop.
- The monitor is the authoritative evidence refresher for `oe2.txt` and `remotecompletion.md`; it reads the live GitHub repo state, local SHA state, workflow evidence, QAUDITS audit status, and exact remote proof gates, then writes the current summary and blockers to both files.
- Evidence coverage requirement: every required artifact and claim must have matching evidence, not just a summary or a passing local test. The monitor enforces the required evidence chain: authenticated GitHub identity, live remote SHA, local/remote parity, branch protection write authority, exact-SHA workflow success, and QAUDITS review completeness.
- QAUDITS policy: `QAUDITS.md` is the repository inventory for required evidence domains. A `NEEDS_REVIEW` or incomplete audit result is treated as a blocker, not a decorative status. The monitor explicitly records audit summaries and preserves them as part of the completion gate.
- Completion gate rule: no remote completion, merge, release, deployment, or Q-version publication claim is valid while `completion_status` is `BLOCKED`, while `remote_matches_local` is false, or while branch protection remains unverified.
- Required files that must be synchronized: `oe2.txt`, `remotecompletion.md`, `MERGE.md`, `RELEASES.md`, `ALLVALIDATIONS.md`, `ALLMDFILESREFS.md`, and the machine-readable evidence artifacts when relevant.
- Safe behavior: the monitor is read-only by default and keeps a strict fail-closed posture. It never inflates a local success into remote proof and never treats a workflow dispatch or token presence as completion authority.
- This automation must continue until all blockers are resolved and the gate is `READY`; otherwise the repo remains deliberately blocked and the evidence remains honest.

## Remote hardening research checkpoint — 2026-10-06T01:51:54Z

### Internal research findings
- The repo already contains the required control points for fail-closed verification in `scripts/live_github_verifier.py`, `scripts/github_auth.py`, `scripts/autonomous_evidence_monitor.py`, and the workflow gate logic in `.github/workflows/auto-merge-automated-pr.yml`.
- The remote-first policy is explicit: `LOCAL_PASS + REMOTE_BLOCKED` remains the only valid state unless GitHub itself confirms authenticated identity, exact SHA, branch protection, required checks, code scanning, and final remote state.
- The current code path distinguishes `auth_verified`, `remote_main_sha`, `remote_matches_local`, `branch_protection_status`, and `exact_sha_successful_workflow`; it does not allow a local-only success to be promoted into a remote completion claim.
- The current workflow evidence for the exact local SHA is split: run `37390478652` failed at step 19 (`Processing Request (Linux)`), while runs `37390464746` and `37390464710` succeeded. That is evidence of separate workflow outcomes, not proof of repository completion.
- The implementation correctly treats `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` as secrets and blocks when no authenticated identity is available. It does not permit bypassing protected branch or code-scanning gates.

### External research findings
- GitHub REST docs classify invalid credentials as `401 Unauthorized`, while insufficient permission or hidden resources may surface as `403 Forbidden` or `404 Not Found` depending on the resource and token capability.
- Branch protection and ruleset endpoints are authenticated APIs; they cannot be treated as readable or writable without a validated token and scope.
- Code scanning is a distinct part of the repository security model. A workflow execution failure is not equivalent to a code-scanning finding, and a successful scan with findings is still a separate policy decision.
- The safe model is therefore: authenticate -> verify token scope -> inspect branch protection/rulesets -> confirm exact SHA -> verify required checks -> check code scanning -> verify PR/merge state -> only then consider deployment or completion.

- Current local HEAD: `7d33581d7e14f169ef659ec37b24baa90c96caef`
- Current remote main: `d0899f4d40a6ab87202234782124991a41a37e65`
- Current remote autosync-backup: `6d925c33f0093035755772137d863618b3818ab0`
- Local branch: `codespace-sturdy-fishstick-69wxr9jjgw5pfrq65`

- Branch protection preflight currently returns `401 Requires authentication`.
- The valid conclusion remains `LOCAL_PASS + REMOTE_BLOCKED`, not `REMOTE_VERIFIED`.
